## 2026-09-27 06:00 UTC — /inventory 3PL vs In-house Cost Comparator (interactive-tool + state-persistence + cross-page-intelligence).

- **What shipped:** New interactive calculator mounted on `/inventory` between the page hero and the static 3PL-vs-in-house / Major-3PLs / Forecasting / CCC cards. The operator enters the in-house cost stack (FTE headcount × loaded $/hr × warehouse lease × inbound freight × misc ops × returns processing time) AND a 3PL quote (pick-pack per order × storage per pallet × receiving per hour) — the panel computes both monthly cost stacks side-by-side, the annualized delta, the break-even monthly order volume above which in-house wins, the ship-time savings (0.5–3.0 days P50), the per-order delta, and assigns a 5-bucket verdict tag (STRONG 3PL / LEAN 3PL / PARITY / LEAN IN-HOUSE / STRONG IN-HOUSE) with a 1-line operator-handoff justification. Defaults match research/05 §3PL-vs-in-house canonical bench (2,500 orders/mo × $75 AOV × 3 FTE × $28/hr loaded × $8,500/mo lease × 12 pallets × ShipBob Mid-Market quoted at $5.10 pick+pack + $35/pallet storage + $40/hr receiving → STRONG 3PL with $4.15/order advantage). State persists to localStorage (`ecom-ops:inventory-cost-comparator:v1`). Copy-report emits a paste-ready markdown handoff. **Cross-page intelligence**: on first mount, the panel reads `ecom-ops:your-store:v1` and pre-fills `monthlyOrders` + `aov` from the operator's Your-store card on Overview; re-hydrates on focus if Overview inputs are updated. Sidebar nav entry was already present at `/inventory`.
- **Why it matters:** `/inventory` was the operator surface with the most gutted interactive coverage — 4 static research tables and 1 hand-curated CCC block, but no way to translate "MY in-house cost + MY 3PL quote" into a unit-economics verdict. The page already linked `Move #12 / research/05` and was adjacent to `/3pl` (which has a path-A/B/C selector scorer), but the gap was the *cost-only comparator* — the thing an operator actually needs to decide "stay in-house or migrate to 3PL". This calculator closes that gap without duplicating the path-selector on `/3pl`. The Your-store overlay means an operator who hasn't tuned their inputs sees numbers generated from their real monthlyOrders + aov, not industry medians.
- **Files added:** `dashboard/src/lib/inventory-cost-comparator.ts` (552 lines, pure rule engine — `computeInventoryCost()` + `validateInventoryInputs()` + `inventoryVerdictTag()` + `renderInventoryCostMarkdown()` + `mergeFromYourStoreForInventory()` + 5-bucket verdict thresholds), `dashboard/src/components/inventory-cost-comparator.tsx` (685 lines, client component with 10 in-house + 4 3PL input fields + side-by-side cost-stack output + 4-metric delta bar + verdict tag + copy report), `dashboard/src/lib/__tests__/inventory-cost-comparator.test.ts` (42 pure-rule-engine assertions — defaults bench, 5 verdict buckets with hand-tuned fixture overrides, break-even math under 4 branches of fixed/variable sign combinations, storage merge + validation + markdown render).
- **Files modified:** `dashboard/app/inventory/page.tsx` (mounted `<InventoryCostComparator />` between the page hero and the static 3PL-vs-in-house card; updated hero copy to "The calculator below turns those benchmarks into YOUR monthly cost stack + a 5-bucket verdict + break-even volume").
- **Verification:** (a) RED-GREEN — feature was absent (`grep -E "Cost Comparator|PathCalculator|verdict" dashboard/app/inventory/page.tsx` → 0 matches) before this tick. (b) GREEN — pure-rule-engine tests **42/42 PASS** (`npx jiti src/lib/__tests__/inventory-cost-comparator.test.ts` → "OK"): defaults match canonical bench (in-house per-order ≈ $9.58, 3PL per-order ≈ $5.43, delta ≈ $4.15); 5 verdict buckets each land in the expected band (STRONG_3PL at default, LEAN_3PL at $7k lease + $6/pick-pack, PARITY at $2k lease + $4.60/pick-pack + 6 pallets, LEAN_INHOUSE at 5 FTE × $18/hr loaded + $7.40/pick-pack, STRONG_INHOUSE at 1 FTE × $18/hr loaded + $16/pick-pack + 2 pallets); break-even = ceil(|fixedDelta / variableDelta|) when signs agree (∞ otherwise); markdown emits all 7 required headers + the verdict label. (c) GREEN — `cd dashboard && NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` SUCCEEDED on FIRST attempt; `/inventory` route grew from `4.77 kB` to `6.65 kB` First Load JS (delta 1.88 kB — pure-cost-comparator client component). (d) `vercel deploy --prod --yes --token <vcp>` SUCCEEDED — deployment-id `dpl_Ds7MCpyYfPDrpsX1A7jp2zAqfW9Q`, hostname `dashboard-nlydluk8s-mattiasadem-5021s-projects.vercel.app`, READY/target=production. ## 2026-09-26 18:37 UTC — /playbooks: per-playbook phased progress tracker (16/30 playbooks decomposed into N actionable phases)

- **What shipped:** New `<PhasedProgress />` component on `/playbooks` between the ROI calculators and the binary "Mark shipped" tracker. Parses each playbook's `## Step-by-step` H3 sub-headings into a tickable checklist at build time (16 playbooks have phases); operator checks off each step as they ship it. State persists to localStorage (`ecom-ops:playbook-phases:v1`) so per-step progress survives reloads and is per-browser. Fleet-wide strip reports "X / N phases · pct%" with copy-report (paste-ready markdown), reset-all, and per-playbook Mark-all-phases / Reset-phases / Open-playbook actions. A "Mark shipped now" affordance surfaces when all phases of a playbook are complete but the binary toggle hasn't been flipped yet (so the operator can still pin shipped-on date + note). Compact `PhasedProgressStrip` card mirrors the binary `ShippedProgressStrip` on `/`.
- **Why it matters:** The shipped-playbooks tracker is binary — operator can't see "65% through PB-04 welcome series, 5 of 9 steps live" without re-reading the playbook markdown. The phased tracker closes that gap: per-step granularity + a fleet-wide rolled-up percentage that answers "what % of the playbook library have I actually built?" The 16-of-30 ratio is honest — playbooks without a `## Step-by-step` section (research docs retrofitted as playbooks, strategy/marketing pieces) are excluded from the denominator so they don't drag the average to 0.
- **Files added:** `dashboard/src/components/phased-progress.tsx` (~440 lines, main + `FleetStrip` + `PlaybookPhasedRow` + `PhasedProgressStrip`), `dashboard/src/lib/playbook-phases.ts` (~190 lines, storage + `togglePhase`/`markAllPhases`/`clearPlaybookPhases`/`clearAllPhasedProgress` + `pctComplete` + `totalProgressPct` + `renderPhasedMarkdown` + intent bands)
- **Files modified:** `dashboard/scripts/parse-content.mjs` (new `playbook.phases` extraction — walk section list, capture H3 sections between Step-by-step H2 and next H2; stable id = `${order}-${slug}`), `dashboard/src/lib/content.ts` (`PlaybookPhase` interface + `Playbook.phases?`), `dashboard/src/lib/content.json` (re-parsed: 30 playbooks, 16 with phases; PB-01 9 phases, PB-02 7 phases, PB-03 24 phases, etc.), `dashboard/app/playbooks/page.tsx` (mount `<PhasedProgress />` above `<ShippedPlaybooksTracker>`), `dashboard/app/page.tsx` (mount `<PhasedProgressStrip />` next to `<ShippedProgressStrip>` on `/`)
- **Verification:** RED-GREEN — feature absent pre-tick. GREEN — `node scripts/parse-content.mjs` re-parsed and emitted `wrote /data/workspace/ecommerce-ops/dashboard/src/lib/content.json — 30 playbooks` (16 with phases). `cd dashboard && NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` SUCCEEDED on first attempt with zero errors and zero warnings; /playbooks bundle emitted at 2.84 kB / 868 kB First Load JS. `vercel deploy --prod --yes` SUCCEEDED — hostname `dashboard-fsjhhl64z-mattiasadem-5021s-projects.vercel.app`, READY/target=production. `vercel alias set … ecommerce-ops-iota.vercel.app` SUCCEEDED in 1s. Move #358 tells the operator when to auto-transfer inventory between warehouses. Move #358.1 tells the operator how long each transfer takes to amortize. Move #358.2 tells the operator when the primary warehouse is at-capacity. Move #358.3 tells the operator when to bypass warehouse entirely via supplier-drop-ship. **None of them tell the operator: "given a 2.5-3.5× Q4 demand multiplier, simulate 5 stress-test scenarios (BFCM-base / BFCM-2x / BFCM-3x / BFCM-supplier-outage / BFCM-warehouse-outage) per region per quarter and pre-position inventory 8 weeks pre-BFCM based on the per-region cost-vs-stockout-cost-vs-holding-cost calculator".** Move #358.4 fills that gap with a 6-pillar per-region-BFCM-stress-test framework + per-region BFCM-peak-demand-forecast-vector 5-dim + per-region pre-positioning-engine 4-tuple + per-region fulfillment-pool-allocation-engine 4-tuple + per-region SLA-target table 3-tier + per-region stress-test-scenario-engine with 5 scenarios + per-region rollback-decision-engine 4-sub-rule R1-R4 + Triple-Whale per-stress-test-event-stream 24-field schema. The 18 pitfalls (P1-P18) cover the most common operator mistakes including P1 (ship-without-per-region-BFCM-peak-demand-forecast-vector → 2.5-3.5× Q4 multiplier missed), P3 (no-3-tier-region-SLA-target-table → AU-cohort sees US-East-SLA-and-fails), P4 (no-stockout-avoidance-value-in-calculator → 30-50% pre-positioning-value-undercounted), P7-R1 (projected-stockout-cost > $50k at day 60 pre-BFCM → engine doesn't expand pool), P11/P12 (no-BFCM-supplier-outage + no-BFCM-warehouse-outage scenario coverage). Year-1 ROI band 5:1-18:1 with default 10:1 at $3M GMV Path B (BFCM-stockout-cost-avoidance $30k-$80k/region/quarter × 2 regions + BFCM-pre-positioning-cost-savings 12-25% × 30%-pre-positioning-eligible-cells + BFCM-delivery-delay-cost-avoidance $15k-$40k/region/quarter × 2 regions + BFCM-customer-LTV-loss-avoidance $20k-$50k/region/quarter × 2 regions + BFCM-re-marketing-cost-avoidance $10k-$25k/region/quarter × 2 regions + BFCM-3PL-overflow-cost-savings $10k-$30k/region/quarter × 2 regions + per-region-stress-test-cost-amortization via Triple-Whale per-stress-test-event-stream = aggregate-savings $170k-$450k/quarter × 4 quarters = $680k-$1.8M/Year / $50k-$80k platform-cost + operator-hours = 5:1-18:1 ratio with default 10:1).

Move #358 tells the operator when to transfer inventory between warehouses. Move #358.1 tells the operator how long each transfer takes to amortize. Move #358.2 tells the operator when the primary warehouse is at-capacity and how to overflow-route. Move #357 tells the operator how many units to buy. **None of them tell the operator: "should we bypass the warehouse entirely and have the supplier ship direct to the customer when supplier-lead-time < transfer-lead-time?".** Move #358.3 fills that gap with a 6-pillar per-SKU-supplier-drop-ship framework + per-SKU-supplier-lead-time-vector 5-dim + per-SKU-transfer-lead-time-vector 4-dim + per-SKU-drop-ship-eligibility-decision-engine 7-condition gate + per-supplier-drop-ship-throughput-vector 4-dim + per-SKU-drop-ship-cost-vs-transfer-cost calculator 4-term math + AUTO-EXECUTE-DROP-SHIP / OPERATOR-APPROVAL / OPERATOR-ESCALATE / REJECT routing + per-SKU-drop-ship-rollback-decision-engine 4-sub-rule R1-R4 + per-supplier risk-tier-scoring-engine + Triple-Whale per-drop-ship-event-stream 22-field schema. The 18 pitfalls (P1-P18) cover the most common operator mistakes including P1 (ship-without-per-SKU-supplier-lead-time-vector → 30d-baseline-missing), P3 (no-supplier-p90-vs-transfer-p90-threshold → 5× stockout at BFCM-peak), P4 (no-supplier-BFCM-peak-capacity-overlay → Q4 throttle), P7 (no-confidence-band-on-decision → operators distrust auto-decisions), P9-R1 (supplier-p90-spike rollback missing → stockouts surge at day 30), P13 (no-Move-#358.2-CLI-band-overlay → AT-CAPACITY-and-CRITICAL escalation missed). ## 2026-09-26 13:25 UTC — /pdp-ab-launch-plan: 30-day always-on PDP A/B launch plan generator (Move #9.5)

- **What shipped:** New route at `/pdp-ab-launch-plan` with a one-click "Generate 30-day launch plan" button. Opens a modal with a 4-week day-by-day checklist (Days 1–30 across W1–W4) generated from the operator's saved PDP A/B calculator inputs (`ecom-ops:playbooks:pdp-ab:v1`) overlaid with their Your-store AOV/orders/margin (`ecom-ops:your-store:v1`). Snapshot block (verdict + annualized ratio + AOV + margin + sessions + tests/month) computed by `forecastPdpAbTest()` — same math as `scripts/pdp_ab_test.py`. Result is paste-ready markdown: copy-to-clipboard OR download `.md` (named `pdp-ab-launch-plan-YYYY-MM-DD.md`). 30 days, 4 weeks, 30 checkbox-able actions covering: tool selection, analytics instrumentation, baseline metrics, hypothesis backlog, variant build, QA across breakpoints, monitoring config, launch, interim read, final readout, Test #2 kickoff, program readout. Sidebar nav entry added (icon ▦).
- **Why it matters:** Closes the gap between "you've tweaked the calculator inputs" and "the 30-day program is actually in your project tracker". The PDP A/B playbook already lists the days in markdown; this turns the playbook into a one-click artifact that drops straight into Linear / Notion / Google Cal / Slack without the operator having to re-type. The Your-store overlay means a DTC operator who hasn't opened the playbook sees the plan generated from their real AOV / margin / monthly orders, not industry medians.
